# THE OAUTH LOGIN FLOW IS UNREACHABLE, AND THE UNBOUNDED-NUMBER SWEEP FOUND FOUR MORE

## OAuth consent is gated by a credential no browser can present

`crates/busbar-oauth2/src/routes.rs:86`, verified:

```rust
.route(id.consent_path(), RouteMethod::Get,  RouteAuth::Admin, consent_screen)
.route(id.consent_path(), RouteMethod::Post, RouteAuth::Admin, consent_submit)
```

The consent screen is **where a browser lands after the `/authorize` redirect** — it is the human
half of the flow. Both its GET and its POST are `RouteAuth::Admin`, the header-only admin
credential. The route immediately above it is `RouteAuth::None`, so this is a deliberate per-route
choice rather than a blanket default.

**A browser following a redirect cannot present an admin header.** In any deployment with a real
admin-auth chain, the OAuth login flow cannot be completed by the person it exists for.

## The unbounded-numeric sweep: one proven defect became five

I asked for every numeric field on the admin PATCH surface to be enumerated and traced, on the
strength of the single `pool_idle_timeout_secs` instance. That is what a systematic sweep is for,
and it found **four more of the same class**:

| field | where it lands | when it bites |
|---|---|---|
| `limits.upstream_request_timeout_secs` | a panicking `Instant + Duration` on the **LLM streaming hot path** (`send.rs:67`) | live |
| `health.default_probe_timeout_secs` | `busbar-llm/src/engine/health.rs:385` | **delayed fuse** — crashes the next restart's freshly-spawned prober, not immediately |
| `advanced.usage_flush_interval_ms` | `busbar-kernel/src/governance/mod.rs:1230` — unvalidated **and falsely classified as live** | **delayed fuse** — widens the governance-spend durability window after a later restart |
| `export/webhook.rs:178` | fourth instance, config-file-reachable rather than PATCH-reachable | boot |

Two of those are **delayed fuses**, which is the more dangerous shape: the operator sets a value,
the API reports success, nothing happens, and the failure arrives at the next restart — by which
time the change and the crash are separated by hours or days and by whoever restarted the process.

**The remedy is one rule, not five fixes:** every numeric on the admin surface declares a bound, and
a value that reaches an arithmetic operation, an allocation or a sleep without one is a gate failure.
That belongs in Phase 1 beside the "a comment asserting a measurement must name its command" rule.

## Two more, and the third sighting of a shipped duplicate

- **`busbar-core-admin/src/verbs.rs:303`** — a complete, shipped-but-dead **duplicate key-mint/rotate
  implementation with its own idempotency cache**. Unreachable today; a latent double-mint the
  moment anything wires a second transport to it. **Third instance of two-implementations-both-shipping**
  (after the two breakers and the two voice planes) — and this one mints credentials.
- **`busbar-core-admin/src/rate.rs:72`** — the admin rate-limit class table never gained the two
  1.6.0 named-map sections (`tools`, `agents`), so full-App-rebuild mutations on those routes get
  **6× the intended rate budget.** A 1.6.0 surface added without its 1.6.0 limit.

---

## AN ORCHESTRATION DEFECT OF MINE, NOW AT THREE INSTANCES

Three lenses have now reported that sub-agents they dispatched **ignored an explicit instruction not
to write files and wrote directly to the shared findings file** — in two cases racing, in one case
the second silently overwriting the first.

All three caught it. The resource lens re-verified twelve quotes and found a directory nothing had
read. The admin lens verified every claim itself and **corrected two factually wrong failure
scenarios** — the health-probe and usage-flush findings had been framed as immediate crashes when
the real mechanism is restart-gated, which is exactly the kind of error that makes a report sound
more alarming than the truth.

**The defect is mine, not theirs:** I told lenses to write one file and left nested forking
available, so a fork inherits "write your findings to X" and races its siblings. The fix for the
next round is structural — **one writer per file, and forks return text to their parent** — and it
goes in the round-4 brief.

The saving grace is that every lens that hit this caught it and said so unprompted. A round where
the sub-agents misbehave and the lens reports it is worth more than a round where they behave
silently, because it tells me which reports were re-verified and which were merely received.

---

# A CLIENT CAN SEND ONE MALFORMED FIELD AND GET FREE INFERENCE

`crates/busbar-llm/src/engine/attempt/assemble.rs:251`, verified by hand. busbar forces
`stream_options.include_usage = true` on streaming OpenAI-family requests so a usage frame always
arrives. The injection has one escape hatch:

```rust
let Some(so_obj) = so.as_object_mut() else {
    // `stream_options` present but not an object: leave the body untouched (the upstream will 400
    // on the malformed field; busbar must not silently reshape a caller's value).
    return payload;
};
```

**The whole defence rests on the comment's parenthesis — an assumption about a third party's
parser.** It holds for OpenAI proper. It does not hold for the OpenAI-*compatible* lanes this
product exists to aggregate (vLLM, Together, Groq, self-hosted), which routinely ignore an unknown
or wrong-typed field and serve the stream normally.

The chain, end to end:
1. client sends `"stream_options": "x"` — a string, not an object
2. busbar returns the payload untouched; **`include_usage` is never injected**
3. a lenient upstream serves the full stream
4. no usage frame arrives
5. busbar's own *"no usage frame ⇒ bill zero"* rule fires
6. **the entire per-token charge for real, delivered output is zero**

There is **no estimation fallback anywhere in the crate** — I checked; the only `fallback` in
`engine/usage.rs` concerns a latency value.

**This is client-triggerable, needs no special credentials, and is one JSON field.** It is the most
directly exploitable money defect in the audit, and the reasoning that permits it is written down as
a safety justification. That makes it the ninth instance of the release's dominant pattern: a
comment asserting a guarantee the code does not hold — here, a guarantee about someone else's server.

## Two more money splits on the LLM plane, both from the two-driver era

- **`unit/admit.rs:173`** — the new step-chain builds a request's `UsageSink` pool key from the
  **raw destination, before Route resolves it**; the legacy driver builds it **after**. So a
  bare-model request's spend lands in a **different accounting bucket depending on which of the two
  live drivers served it.**
- **`native_ingress.rs:602`** — MCP-sampling-triggered completions are hard-wired to the legacy
  `run_gauntlet` path and **can never reach the new durable ledger book** that direct HTTP traffic
  posts to by default. A permanent two-books-disagree gap on an entire traffic class.

**And the oracle that exists to catch exactly this is blind to both.** `unit/tests/chain.rs:458` —
the crate's own legacy-vs-chain money-parity check — tracks **only aggregate totals**, never
pool/bucket attribution, and **never touches the new ledger book at all.** A parity oracle that
compares sums cannot see a misattribution that preserves the sum.

*(Honest resolution of an open question I posed: `Metered.refund`/`Walk::refund()` and the live
`finish_admitted` refund compute the **identical** predicate today — same `charged` flag, same
response status. They are redundant, not conflicting. But they are uncoupled, so future drift would
be caught by neither suite.)*

---

# THE INSTRUMENTS, SECOND OPINION: 51 FINDINGS, AND TWO BLOCKING GATES THAT CANNOT FAIL

A second, independent opus lens over `xtask/src/gates/` — 92 files, 82,309 lines, all opened —
returned **51 findings (21 HIGH)**, converging with the first lens at the same HIGH count on the
same slice. It re-verified all 51 quotes after assembly and corrected three. Two results stand out
because both gates **block CI**.

**`ci_umbrella.rs:632` — the gate that decides whether CI can fail polices the ledger and never the
judge.** All fourteen rows are computed from the umbrella job's `needs:`, its `env.RESULTS` block
and the workflow's job/`if:` map. I checked what it actually reads: `RESULTS`. **Nothing reads the
umbrella's `steps:`** — and the steps are where the verdict lives (`ci.yml:3866-3871`:
`if [ "$fails" -ne 0 ]; then … exit 1`).

Exploit, stated by the finder and trivial: change that `exit 1` to `exit 0`, or replace the loop
with `run: true`. `RESULTS` is untouched — 28 well-shaped rows, every one in `needs`, all floors
clear — **the gate stays GREEN while CI's umbrella stops failing on anything.**

**`teller_steps.rs:913` — a blocking CI step prints its denominator and never checks it.** Verified
verbatim:

```rust
"ROOT-GATING: the shipped legs are {legs}; {considered} gating plane x step cell(s) are theirs to drive."
…
if red.is_empty() { println!("GREEN: every gating step on every shipped root leg…"); return 0; }
```

An empty shipped-leg set produces an empty `red`, so it prints GREEN and exits 0. Remove the five
`root-*` features from `Cargo.toml:192` and a blocking gate passes over nothing. **That is Law 8's
exact shape — a measurement that states its denominator and never proves it is non-empty** — sitting
in a step that gates promotion.

The rest of the 51 run the same way: `shared-route` scans a kind that does not exist (both lenses
found this); `ports-only`'s eight rows search for a **deleted crate's path spelling**;
`Excuse::ReleaseScript` proves a gate is named in a script **nothing runs**;
`field-inventory:both-directions` has a FAIL arm **no input can reach**; `reachability` scores an
**absent** unit module as PASS; `hot-path-*` matches `harness = false` anywhere in a manifest with
three bench blocks; and `ship-ready`'s posture dereferences a table it can never be in, which is why
`gate --all` is **permanently red** — the standing red this plan has been treating as debt is partly
a bug in the gate.

---

# CORRECTION: THE FIVE MCP CLIENT FAILURES ARE ONE DEFECT AND A BLIND HARNESS

I reported five hostile-server scenarios as client security defects — that busbar detects neither a
rug-pull, nor an undeclared capability, nor an outputSchema violation, nor a bad icon scheme, nor an
evil elicitation prompt. **That was the conformance suite's verdict, restated by me without going to
the code.** The round-3 lens went to the code. The picture is different and narrower.

**One is real, and its cause is a path asymmetry — verified by hand:**

`crates/busbar-mcp/src/mcp/tasks.rs` — the async SEP-2663 task-completion path contains **zero
references to `outputschema`**. The synchronous path validates correctly at `method.rs:2049`
(`super::outputschema::check(structured, schema)`). **So a task-capable tool with a published
`output_schema` gets no structured-output validation at all, while the identical synchronous call
does.** Same contract, two paths, one checked.

*(And a narrower one beside it: even the sync check only runs when `structuredContent` is present,
so an upstream that publishes a schema and then omits the field entirely escapes the MUST check.)*

**The other four did not reproduce.** Rug-pull digest-pinning, MRTR deny-by-default gating and the
outputSchema check itself all read as correct, well-engineered code on the HTTP client path. No
defect was found behind the undeclared-capability or evil-elicitation claims.

**The likely explanation is a harness that is wrong about the product it tests.**
`testing/mcp-conformance/` drives everything through an HTTP mirror, with at most one `tools/call`
per scenario, on the premise that busbar's MCP client speaks a single transport and has no child
supervisor. I checked: `crates/busbar-mcp/src/mcp/client/stdio.rs` is **582 lines of code with 31
spawn sites** — a real, production, config-validated subprocess supervisor. **The battery never
exercises it.**

So the honest statement is: **one confirmed product defect, and a conformance suite that cannot see
half the client's transport surface.** Both are items; neither is the five-headed security finding I
reported.

**Why I got it wrong, and the rule that follows.** A failing test is evidence that *something* is
wrong — it is not evidence about *what*. I treated five red rows as five defects because the rows
were named after behaviours. That is the same error as trusting a failure message over the code
(item 106) and trusting a grep over a positive control. **A red test names a symptom; the defect is
wherever the code says it is.** This is now the eighth instance in the release of prose asserting
something the code contradicts — and the first where the prose was a *test harness's* own
description of the system under test, which is the most dangerous place for it.

### The rest of the MCP round

| sev | where | finding |
|---|---|---|
| MED | `client/catalogue.rs:70` | the MCP `icons` field is unmodeled everywhere — client parsing, config grammar, server rendering. Harmless **today** only because busbar drops non-allowlisted upstream fields; no scheme gate exists if that changes |
| MED | `busbar-plane-mcp/src/claims.rs:118` | the plane's compile-time boot-collision `Claim` **hardcodes `/mcp`** while the real mount path is operator-configurable via `canonical_uri` — self-documented in the file's own header as unresolved |
| MED | `client/peer.rs:312` | on the stdio transport **every granted authority ask is unconditionally `Unsatisfiable`**, including `sampling`, which has a working satisfier on HTTP. Config validation never warns that `transport: stdio` + `grants.sampling` + a full sampling policy can never fire |
| LOW | `client/roots.rs:118` | `grants.elicitation: true` passes validation with no satisfier anywhere — an intentional, recorded waiver |

`peer.rs:312` is worth pairing with the harness gap: the stdio transport is both **the one the
conformance battery never drives** and the one where every authority ask silently fails.

---

# C2 IS REFUTED FOR A THIRD KIND: A DROPPED-IN PLANE CAN TAKE MONEY AND CANNOT GIVE IT BACK

Round 2 reported the `LEASES` map as "mintable over FFI with no forced release." Round 3 found the
sharper truth and I verified every part of it:

**The C-ABI `PlaneHostVtable` has no lease-release verb at all.**

- `busbar-plugin/src/hot/host.rs:722,724` declares exactly two slots: `cost_reserve`,
  `cost_settle`. The stub at `:983,984` has the same two. `plane_host/vtable.rs:121,122` wires the
  same two. **There is no `cost_close` slot.**
- The function exists — `cost_host.rs:118` `pub(crate) fn close_lease` — and is `pub(crate)`, so it
  is unreachable from the other side of the ABI.
- **Positive control, and it is the clincher:** `busbar-voice` *does* close its leases, at
  `runtime/metering.rs:218` and `:417`, via the `MeteringHost` **Rust trait**. Its own doc at `:179`
  says `cost_close` *"fires on ANY return path (including a panic unwinding through `run()`)"* and
  is idempotent.

So the compiled-in fold has a release verb, uses it, and is careful enough to make it panic-safe and
idempotent. **The dropped-in fold has no such verb in its vocabulary.** A dlopen'd plane can reserve
money and settle money and can never release a lease it opened.

**That is C2's third disjunct — "any kind whose two folds differ" — and it is money.**

### C2's scoreboard: refuted for three of the seven kinds

| kind | how the two folds differ |
|---|---|
| **export** | a dropped-in export plugin **cannot be named in a config file**; core hard-errors on any module name outside its four built-ins, and `open_export` has zero callers |
| **store** | `priced_from_ms` has no ABI adapter, so a dropped-in v2 store **merges a split day back into one row and prices it all at the midnight card** while the compiled-in store gets it right |
| **plane** | the vtable has **no `cost_close`**, so a dropped-in plane cannot release a money lease while the compiled-in path does so on every return path including a panic |

Three kinds, three different mechanisms, all money or configuration. The claim is not marginally
false; it is false in the ordinary case for nearly half the taxonomy.

---

## ROUND 3 RESOURCE: FIVE MEDIUM, AND AN HONEST NEGATIVE WORTH MORE THAN THEM

The lens read **49 previously-unread production files in full (18,580 LOC)** — exactly the set
round 2's own `unread` key named — split across six readers, with every finding re-verified by
direct grep before acceptance.

It was told to hunt a fifth instance of the confirmed *removed-not-marked* pattern. **It looked
across all six groups and reported it did not find one.** That is the most valuable sentence in the
report: a lens that returns a clean negative on a pattern it was primed to find is a lens whose
positives can be trusted. It found *close cousins* instead — uncapped rather than unmarked — and
said so rather than stretching the label.

| where | finding |
|---|---|
| `plane/handle_engine.rs:141` | **active (non-terminal) handles have no cap at all** — only the terminal population is bounded, so a caller that keeps handles perpetually touched grows the map without limit |
| `plane_host/dispatch.rs:121` | the durable work-handle registry is never swept **and its happy path never removes the entry either** — it leaks on the successful path as well as the abandoned one |
| `plane_host/trust_anchor.rs:37` | no cap or eviction, unlike its declared sibling `identity.rs`, **which was given a FIFO cap to fix this exact accumulation bug** |
| `plane_host/trust.rs:76` | the verify-freshness `fresh` map only grows; its siblings `leading`/`inflight` are cleaned up properly, and the primitive it is modelled on (`trust::verify::VerifyGate`) has a `retain()` this cache lacks |

Two of those — `trust_anchor` and `trust` — are cases where **the correct fix already exists beside
the defect**, applied to a sibling. That is the same shape as the two breakers and the two
`reserved_nanos` copies: the tree knows the answer and did not propagate it.

---

# THE BANK-AUDITOR ANSWER: THE BOOKS SHIP WITH NO WORKING TAMPER DETECTION

The money lens read **52 of 52 files** — every previously-unread test file in the three money crates
(14,689 LOC) plus the unread `usage/` and `cost/` sources. It was sent to find the sibling of
`residual`'s wrapping verifier. **It found that the sibling is not one checker. It is all of them.**

## Every money-side tamper detector has zero production callers

- `verify::verify` — the ledger verifier
- `CheckpointAnchor::anchor` — the checkpoint-vs-anchor rollback detector
- `recompute` / `recheck`
- `adjusting_entries` / `record_repricing`
- all four `AuditChain` verifiers
- **`AmendChain` — the entire amendment journal, which the design names as the ONLY documented way
  a recorded money figure may legitimately change**

I verified the two most load-bearing myself, and the check is subtle enough that a coarse grep
reports the opposite:

- **`AmendChain`: 9 non-test references, every one inside `busbar-kernel-audit` itself** — its own
  `pub struct`, its own `Default`, its own `impl`, two internal `digest_of` uses, and one
  `pub use` in that crate's `lib.rs`. **Nothing outside the defining crate names it.**
- **`verify`: `fn verify(` matches five places**, and four are unrelated trait methods on other
  types (`units_mcp.rs:664`, `units_voice.rs:1462`, `kernel.rs:959`, `:1132`). Only
  `ledger/src/verify.rs:113` is the ledger's, and nothing calls it. **Counting those four as
  callers would have produced the opposite conclusion** — the same "a name is not a thing" trap
  that has now cost this release seven false starts.

## The one live edit-detector is defeated by the person it guards against

`Checkpoint::body_hash_verifies` is wired (5 references). It is a **keyless SHA-256** over the body.
Anyone who can alter the stored figure can recompute the hash beside it. It detects corruption. It
does not detect tampering, which is the property the audit chain exists to provide.

**Stated plainly, because this is the question a bank auditor asks first:** the money books carry a
signed audit chain, an amendment journal, a checkpoint anchor and four verifiers — and **not one of
them runs.** The only live integrity check is one a tamperer updates as part of tampering.

## A 64-case money test that cannot fail

`settle_tests.rs:406` is a 64-case "byte-neutrality" sweep. It asserts the overdraft column against
`posted.overdraft()`, **which is `0` in all 64 cases — including the 28 where `used > reserved`**,
i.e. precisely the input that makes the books not balance. A sweep that asserts a constant over the
one variable that matters.

**And the coupling is real in production, not a fixture artifact.** `teller.rs:1323` spends the
hold by `run.meter.total()`; `teller.rs:1346` settles the settlement table's `amount`. Two different
numbers. So `Posted.overdraft` and `Posted.settled` derive from different figures, and `residual`
reads non-zero on **ordinary** ends — not just adversarial ones.

## The ten previously-documented items: all still true, two worse

Round 2 skipped ten money items because in-tree docs already carried them with citations. **A
citation is not a verification** — several findings this session were documented-and-stale — so they
were re-checked at HEAD. All ten hold. Two have got worse and are now items in their own right:

- **`MeterPolicyConfig` is never populated from configuration.** Both production sites pass
  `::default()`, so pool expansions and lane prices are permanently empty and the *"post the
  cheaper lane"* rule **degenerates to an alphabetical tie-break.** Lane selection is not choosing
  the cheaper lane; it is choosing the earlier letter.
- The **truncating multiply-before-divide tier arithmetic** is still live at
  `busbar-kernel/src/cost.rs:286`, under that file's own `allow(dead_code)`.

---

# STREAMING: A PHONE CALL THAT CANNOT WORK, AND IS BILLED ANYWAY

Three HIGH, two of them money, all verified by hand.

## The caller's audio is silently discarded, and the call still settles usage

`busbar-voice`'s telephony endpoint `/v1/realtime/telephony/{call_id}` is live, mounted, governed
and provider-dialing. It feeds raw client bytes straight into `OpenAiRealtimeCodec::read_up`, which
understands only OpenAI's `type`-tagged JSON. **Real Twilio Media Streams frames are `event`-tagged**,
so every one of them reaches the match arm I read at `ir/codec/mod.rs:667`:

```rust
_ => Vec::new(),
```

An unknown frame shape produces an empty event vector. Silently. **The caller's audio never reaches
the model.**

The purpose-built decoder that belongs in between exists and is tested — and has **zero production
callers**. I checked: `TwilioEnvelope`'s only two non-test references in the whole tree are its own
`pub struct` declaration and its own `impl` block (`topology/twilio.rs:154,156`). Positive control
on the identical search: `OpenAiRealtimeCodec` has **65**.

**The session still opens, still dials the provider, and still bills real settled usage** for
one-way model output on a call that cannot function. A customer is charged for a phone call in
which nobody could hear them.

## The session cost ceiling is documented, plumbed, and never compared to anything

`runtime/mod.rs:67` declares `session_max_secs` as *"the hard session wall-clock ceiling
(`streams.session_max_secs:`) **the pump enforces**"*, default 3600s. I traced every reference: a
doc comment, a field declaration, a default fn, a serde attribute, and four plumbing assignments.
**Not one comparison against elapsed time anywhere in the tree.**

The one thing named as its enforcer — the sweep loop at `session.rs:455-475` — only expires stale
tool-call waits, an unrelated concern.

**An operator who sets this ceiling to bound worst-case session cost gets no enforcement at all.**
On a plane that bills by the second, with the abandoned-session leak already confirmed, that is the
difference between a bounded and an unbounded bill.

## And the safety gate the dual-plane comment leans on cannot see the plane it is cited for

`busbar-plane-streaming/src/register.rs:28`'s own doc says the boot's cross-plane claim-overlap
check (`seal_claims`) is what makes co-registering `busbar-voice` and `StreamingPlane` safe.

`plane_claims()` (`busbar/src/root/registry.rs:241-261`) is built **only** from `PlaneMeta::CLAIMS`
on the five new planes. `busbar_voice::PLANE_DECL` uses an entirely different, older claim
mechanism and **is never imported into `registry.rs` at all.** The check structurally cannot see
busbar-voice's audience claim on `/v1/realtime`, so *"it would have tripped the overlap check"* is
false comfort for precisely the pair it is offered about.

---

## THE SHAPE THAT HAS NOW APPEARED SEVEN TIMES

A comment states a guarantee; the mechanism it names cannot deliver it; nothing re-checks the
claim. This is no longer an observation, it is the release's dominant defect class:

| the claim | the reality |
|---|---|
| `Cargo.toml:263` — `root-llm` "moved no cell, every money class byte-identical" | moves a money cell by 472,560,000 µ |
| `Cargo.toml:309` — `root-admin` "the whole 890-cell owed golden read the same either way" | moves two owed ledger cells |
| `field_inventory.rs:84` — a dialect registered with no schema "is a hard error" | all 7 rows stay PASS |
| `layout_golden.rs:596` — a resize "is a MAJOR airlock break — do not re-seed" | re-seeded on a minor bump |
| `conformance:freshness` — "a pass carried over from an older sha is RED" | passing at 647 commits stale |
| `runtime/mod.rs:67` — the ceiling "the pump enforces" | never compared to anything |
| `register.rs:28` — `seal_claims` makes the pair safe | cannot see one of the pair |

**Phase 1 gets one more item from this, and it is mechanical:** a comment that asserts a
measurement or a guarantee must name the command that reproduces it, and that command joins the
gate set. Seven instances is not carelessness by seven authors — it is a missing rule.

---

# C2 IS NOW REFUTED FOR A SECOND KIND — AND THE AIRLOCK BROKE ITS OWN FROZEN RULE

## The store kind: the two folds price a day differently

`priced_from_ms` is **money**. The engine makes it part of the metering accrual key
(`governance/state.rs:893,899`, verified), so a mid-day rate-card edit splits the day's cell in two
— which is the whole point of the field.

It was added to `MeteringDelta`/`MeteringRow` with `#[serde(default)]` and **no `abi_version`
bump**, while `supported_abi("store")` still admits v2 — *every published first-party store*.

So a dropped-in v2 store has no such column, merges both halves back into one row, and returns
`priced_from_ms: 0`. **The admin read then prices the whole day at the midnight card.** The
compiled-in store (`store-memory`) keys on the field correctly.

**Same plugin kind, two folds, two different invoices.** That is C2's third disjunct — *"any kind
whose two folds differ"* — and it is money.

The positive control is damning, and I checked it: `legacy_usage_wire()` guards **5** call sites
(`put_usage`, `add_usage`, `get_usage`) and is **absent from `add_metering`**. The adapter pattern
exists, is used three times beside this, and was not applied to the one that carries a price.

## The airlock: a MINOR bump resized an interior field, and nothing can refuse it

`ABI_MINOR` 20→21 changed `BuildCtx.host_ctx` from `*mut c_void` (8 bytes) to a `#[repr(C)]
HostCtx` (16), shifting `config_ptr`, `config_len`, `resolved_refs_ptr` and `resolved_refs_len` by
8 bytes. `ABI_MAJOR` stayed at 1.

Verified, both sides:
- **`abi_minor` is compared in ZERO places.** Positive control: `abi_major` is compared in one
  (`lib.rs:141`). `check_preamble` refuses on magic and major only.
- **The repo's own layout golden records the resize** across `ec21d27f0`: `config_ptr 32→40`,
  `__size 64→72` — in the same commit that bumped only the minor.
- **And the golden's own assert message states the rule that commit broke.**
  `crates/busbar-plugin/tests/layout_golden.rs:596`: *"If it is a REORDER/RESIZE/INSERT, that is a
  MAJOR airlock break — do not re-seed…"*. It was re-seeded.

**Consequence:** a minor-20 plane passes every check and then reads its config pointer out of
`HostCtx`'s generation and kind bytes. That is memory corruption across the plugin boundary, on the
seam C2 exists to guarantee.

**Stated honestly, as the finder did:** `open_plane` has no in-tree production caller yet, so this
is latent rather than live. It is still a hard blocker, because the whole of Wave 5 is about giving
it one — landing that caller without fixing this ships the corruption.

**The remedy is structural, not a version number.** `abi_minor` must be compared by something, and
the layout golden must refuse a re-seed whose diff is a reorder, resize or insert rather than
printing a sentence about it.

## Five MEDIUMs, all the same shape the round-2 brief predicted

Every one is "a field was added and the other side never reads it" — the pattern proven twice in
round 2 and now found five more times: the hook half of the endpoint ABI (`HookRequest::Routes`/
`Endpoint` — the SDK answers, `DlopenPolicy` never asks, while `export.rs:137` *does*);
`SecretRequest::deadline_ms` (forwarded by the SDK, hardcoded `None` by the one host producer); the
#85 observability envelope (universal by declaration, `drain_observations` on `ExportHandler`
alone); `PlaneDecl.admin_routes`/`openapi` (no slot reader in `DynPlane`, while the compiled-in
native equivalents *are* called); and the log bridge, which `ffi_thread.rs:58` claims runs "per
load" and is emitted by neither `export_plane!` nor `wire_up_plane`.

---

## KERNEL: AN OPERATOR CAN CLEAR A QUARANTINE AND BE TOLD IT WORKED WHEN IT DID NOT

Two more from the kernel subsystems no round-2 lens was pointed at (confirmed unread first, by
parsing all 19 round-2 files for path matches).

**`trust/mod.rs:376` — `Approval::approve` walks only the OBSERVED capability set, so the `removed`
drift axis is never settled.** An A2A agent whose upstream drops a skill goes `Quarantined`; the
operator calls `POST /agents/{id}/approve`, gets **200 `OUTCOME_APPLIED`**, and the agent is **still
undelegable, permanently.** The only API that clears that axis — `reject_capability` — has zero
production callers.

**`export/webhook.rs:87` — tightening a disclosure bound silently does not apply.** Request-log
sinks (URL, auth header, and the resolved `Projection`) are `OnceLock`-frozen by the single boot
call to `export::configure`. The live `PUT /admin/export/{name}` route reports a restart requirement
only for new plugin *route paths*. So adding, re-pointing, or **narrowing** a sink answers "applied
live" and takes effect at the next restart — and `projection.rs` defines a projection as a
disclosure bound. An operator narrowing what leaves the building is told it worked.

---

# THE INSTRUMENTS AUDIT: 21 HIGH, 100% COVERAGE — AND THE MONEY GATE CANNOT SEE THE MONEY DEFECT

The instruments lens read **90 of 90 files, 78,680 of 80,042 lines** — the only lens in the audit at
full coverage — and it **built and ran** the gates rather than reading them, which turned four
findings from argument into measurement. **43 findings: 21 HIGH, 22 MEDIUM.** Every quote re-checked
against `file:line`, zero mismatches.

This is the most consequential slice in the release, because every other green on record is reported
by this code.

## THE ONE TO READ FIRST: the money gate is blind to the money defect, three ways over

`no-float-money` carries a row whose title is *"a count is read with a silent zero default instead
of a refusal (#81)"*. The codec audit independently found exactly that defect —
`read_count_u64(..).unwrap_or(0)` billing an unreadable token count as $0, in all six dialects. The
row cannot see it. I verified each link:

1. **Wrong vocabulary.** `NUMBER_ACCESSORS` is `["as_u64","as_i64","as_f64","as_u128","as_i128"]` —
   raw `serde_json` accessors. The codec reads through a **named helper**,
   `crate::usage_count::read_count_u64`, so no accessor appears at the call site.
2. **The helper's own file is excluded.** `SUPERSEDED_SEAM` is literally
   `crates/busbar-llm-codec/src/usage_count.rs` — the gate skips the one file where the accessor
   does appear.
3. **The helper is the correct one.** `read_count_u64` *refuses* an unreadable count by returning
   `None`; it is the `.unwrap_or(0)` at the call site that converts that refusal into free money.
   The gate is looking for a bad read, and the read is good — the discard is the defect.

**So the single row that exists to catch "#81: never a silent zero" is structurally incapable of
seeing the release's clearest instance of it.** That is Law 0 landing on the money path, and it is
the strongest argument in the plan for why Phase 1 precedes Phase 2.

## THE FOUR THAT WERE MEASURED, NOT ARGUED

| | |
|---|---|
| `conformance_sync/render.rs:310` | `conformance:freshness` claims *"a pass carried over from an older sha is RED"* — and **derives the release commit from the thing it is judging**. Measured live: a row passing over ten verdicts **647 commits stale** |
| `construction/selftest.rs:1052` | **both `forbid-unsafe` RED proofs come back GREEN when run.** The plant strips `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` from **every held crate of the kind**, and every covered row stays green. The gate cannot detect its own total defeat |
| `construction/selftest.rs:539` | the RED proof covering the **entire fourteen-row LOC-ceiling battery** demands a `naming` string that appears nowhere in the gate's output |
| `construction` loc-ceilings | two rows pass over **subjects that do not exist** |

## THE REST OF THE 21, BY SHAPE

**Denominator is the subject** — `teller_steps.rs:151`: *every* population (plane roster, step
roster, cell denominator, root-leg set) is read out of `qa/teller-steps.json`, **the very file under
judgement**. Law 8 in its purest form.

**Empty denominators** — `inventory_coverage.rs:88`: **31 of the 40 rows** of the "40-row coverage
matrix" are bound to an empty family list and return `UNMAPPED` on every possible input.
`kind_isolation.rs:2734`: the banned-needle list is populated for **3 of 21 kinds**.
`kind_isolation.rs:3236`: a refusal iterates crates of kind `"control"`, which is not one of the 21
kinds, so the population is always empty. `design_bindings/tables.rs:620`: the only path by which a
binding's NOTE can refuse it reads an **empty static**.

**Rules that `continue` past their own subject** — `config_schema/classify.rs:313`: the arm catching
`deny_unknown_fields` or `transparent` being flipped — *which operator documents parse* — `continue`s
on **every node, every flag, every run**. `:275`: the arm catching a hand-written `Deserialize`
quietly accepting input it used to reject returns early whenever the baseline node has no refusal.

**Scan roots narrower than the invariant** — `structure_lint/plane_store.rs:88`: *"a plane never
holds the power to forge the audit chain"* judges only `busbar-kernel/src/plane`, and a production
plane trust-state sink lives outside it. `structure_lint/census.rs:109`: a row existing to catch *"a
SECOND spelling of a wire word"* is scoped to a tree that excludes the directory where the second
spelling sits.

**Selftests that cannot bite** — five cases demand offender strings **no rule in the gate can
emit**; four plant into `crates/busbar-plane-admin/`, a directory **with no `Cargo.toml`**, which
every relevant rule keys on; two plant dependency edges onto **packages that do not exist**; one
plants an empty overlay that `inert_plant` refuses before the gate ever runs.

**And `plane_abi_neutrality.rs:233`** derives the tree's plane keys by grepping `pub const
PLANE_DECL` — **a grammar zero `busbar-plane-*` crates use.**

## WHAT THIS DOES TO THE PLAN

Phase 1 was sized at ~16 agents against items 88/89/90 and the C1 kind hole. It is now **21 HIGH +
22 MEDIUM on top of that**, and several are structural (a trait signature, a scan root, a
denominator source) rather than a missing test case.

**It also settles the ordering argument beyond debate.** Wave 0's preamble said *"nothing below this
line can be trusted until this is done."* That was a judgement; it is now a measurement. The money
gate cannot see a confirmed money defect, the unsafe gate cannot see its own removal, and a
freshness gate is 647 commits stale while reporting PASS.

---

# THE C1 SHARP TEST HAS BEEN RUN. C1 IS FALSE — AND THE FAILURE IS CHEAP.

Part 0 calls add-a-dialect-then-diff *"the command that confirms or falsifies the entire premise."*
It has now been run. A seventh dialect (`mistral`) was built end to end, and **the shipped binary
reports it**: `must be one of: anthropic, openai, gemini, bedrock, responses, cohere, mistral`.
That string comes from `known_protocols()` after the composition root folds `DECLS` — the same
registry the six live in. `cargo build --workspace` green. The dialect is real, not a no-op.

**Verdict: 17 files — 10 plugin, 7 CORE. C1 is false.**

### But read the shape before reading the number

- **Phase A** — the codec, reader, writer, handler, registration and arrival wiring: 11 files, only
  1 of them core. Across **596 gate rows, exactly 2 changed**, and both are the same rule
  (`legacy-reach`, a *symbol-count ratchet on the composition root*).
- **Phase B** — all 17 files: **0 rows changed.** Row-for-row identical to baseline.

**So `gate --all` notices a seventh dialect through exactly one rule out of 596.**

**The code side of C1 is nearly true.** The codec, reader/writer/handler, arrivals and metering
table all live in the plane's own crates. What breaks C1 is not architecture — it is **pinned
census**: seven hand-maintained rosters of "the six dialects" and five hand-typed *counts* that one
new claim rung renumbers. Every one of them is a number somebody typed. **That is a data-modelling
problem, not an architecture one, and it is far cheaper than the verdict sounds.**

### The 7 core files a dialect forces

| file | what forces it |
|---|---|
| `busbar/src/root/units_llm.rs` | the root's `BODY_INGRESS`/arrival twin must spell every dialect |
| `busbar/src/root/tests/registry.rs` | **6 pinned counts + a pinned 50-entry sealed-claim order**, and two test functions whose NAMES encode counts had to be renamed (`…forty_eight_claims` → `…forty_nine…`) |
| `qa/construction.toml` | `legacy-reach` ratchet 17→18 — **a dialect addition is structurally a ratchet VIOLATION**, on a rule whose own doc says the total "may go DOWN and never up" |
| `xtask/src/gates/field_inventory.rs` | `const DIALECTS: [&str; 6]` |
| `qa/field-schemas/<d>.json` · `qa/field-inventory.json` · `qa/field-coverage.missing` | a new schema, a regenerated artifact (+285 lines), 40 new pinned field ids |

Plus, forced by other CI jobs and not yet landed: the shadow-oracle golden cell
`boot.refusal__BOOT-020__validate.json` (which pins that stderr byte-for-byte and now diverges,
needing an `accepted-differences.json` entry), `conformance/{registry.toml,manifest.json}`,
`proof-manifest.py` (**four** rosters), `proto-deletion-gate.sh`, `plane-grep-gate.sh`,
`assets/readme/data.json` and the README's 6×6 matrix, and the kernel's own
`proto/tests/registry_builtins.rs`.

### FOUR INSTRUMENT DEFECTS THE TEST EXPOSED, each with a positive control

1. **`field-inventory` advertises the guarantee it does not provide.** Its doc at
   `field_inventory.rs:84` says a dialect *"registered with no schema file is a hard error rather
   than a silently smaller inventory."* In Phase A the dialect was fully registered and printed by
   the shipped binary **with no schema file — and all 7 rows stayed PASS.** The row claims the two
   sets are "EQUAL, both ways"; **both are hardcoded consts and neither reads the registry**, so it
   cannot see the half it calls load-bearing. (The inverse works: schema-without-registration reds
   all 7.)
2. **`plane-purity:dialect` cannot see a new dialect.** Planting a literal in
   `busbar-kernel/src/lib.rs`: clean **17 hits**, `"cohere"` **18** (caught), `"mistral"` **17**
   (invisible). `lean-core` behaves identically: 14 / 15 / 14. Both carry their own `DIALECTS[6]`.
3. **The test written to pin the operator-visible order is a PREFIX match.**
   `cli_validate.rs:1035` is `all.contains("must be one of: anthropic, …, cohere")`, so appending
   `, mistral` **passes**. The one instrument that does catch it is a shadow-oracle golden cell in
   a different CI job.
4. **`cargo xtask gate field-inventory --write` does not exist** — and it is the command printed in
   the gate's own FAIL message AND in the generated artifact's header. `cli.rs:290` has `--write`
   arms for `design-bindings` and `construction` only; everything else exits 2. The artifact had to
   be regenerated by re-implementing `render()` by hand.

**Item 5 is answered.** It estimated "10 dialect rosters". Measured: **7 rosters + 5 hand-typed
counts**, enumerated above. That list IS the C1 worklist.

---

## ROUND 3 — first return: a task waiting on a human is killed at 24 hours

`crates/busbar-a2a/src/taskstore.rs:308` — the retention sweep's abandonment ceiling **durably and
irreversibly cancels any non-terminal task idle for more than exactly 24 hours**, including a task
PAUSED in `input-required` or `auth-required` — i.e. one that is idle precisely because it is
waiting for a person to answer.

The failure is ordinary rather than exotic: a request for human input raised on a Friday afternoon
is cancelled before Monday, and the cancellation is durable, so the answer arrives to a task that
no longer exists. A2A's whole point is long-running delegated work with humans in the loop; a
24-hour hard ceiling on "idle" treats waiting-for-a-human as abandonment.

---

## RESOURCE: SIX MORE HIGH, INCLUDING A LEASE MINTED OVER FFI WITH NO RELEASE

The resource lens re-ran with real coverage (66 of 122 production files, 49,852 LOC) and returned
**14 findings, 6 HIGH** — up from 2 on the thin first pass. Every quote was verified against the
pinned tree by the reporting agent before it reported.

| sev | where | finding |
|---|---|---|
| HIGH | `busbar-kernel-egress/src/attempt.rs:354` | **`Transport::close()` is called only on the success path.** Every non-success exit leaks the dialled connection — an OS child process on stdio, a live task and socket on gRPC/WS. A flapping upstream leaks one process per failed attempt |
| HIGH | `busbar-kernel/src/plane_host/egress.rs:727` | the documented "bounded timeout" backstop ends in an **unbounded `join.join()`** on a thread that can wedge in DNS resolution. The bound is on the wrong side of the join |
| HIGH | `busbar-kernel-egress/src/trust/net.rs:633` | the `Resolver` trait **has no way to carry a deadline at all** — so the timeout above cannot be fixed at the call site; it needs a trait change |
| HIGH | `busbar-kernel/src/plane/handle_engine.rs:842` | a per-handle mutex is **held across an untimed store round-trip**, amplified by an inline sweep under the same guard |
| HIGH | `busbar-kernel/src/plane_host/trust.rs:76` | `VerifyCache.fresh` is a **process-global, insert-only cache with no eviction** |
| **HIGH** | `busbar-kernel/src/plane_host/cost_host.rs:41` | **the `LEASES` map is mintable over the FFI boundary with no forced release.** A plugin can take a money lease and never give it back, and nothing reclaims it. This is the hold protocol's defect at the ABI — the fourth distinct place the same shape appears |
| MED | `plane/handle_engine.rs:143` | `max_retained` bounds terminal handles only, never active ones |
| MED | `plane_host/journal.rs:1015`, `:463` | unbounded per-scope journal accumulation, plus an ABI shape that can disable the cap |
| MED | `plane_host/dispatch.rs:211` | the durable work-handle registry has exactly **one** removal site, unreachable for a zero-TTL or never-resumed handle |
| MED | `plane_host/identity_admit.rs:39` | same mint-over-ABI / no-release-verb shape as `cost_host`: `take()` is `pub(crate)` and not exposed over the ABI |
| MED | `ingress/duplex_ws.rs:79` | the WS reader's only await has **no timeout and no idle or ping liveness check anywhere in the codebase**, so a silent-but-open peer pins the task and its state indefinitely |
| LOW | `plane_host/trust_anchor.rs:37` | the unbounded-registry bug its sibling `identity.rs` already needed a fix for — latent today |

**The pattern, now at four instances:** a resource is minted and the release path is either absent,
`pub(crate)`, or on the success branch only. `cost_host`'s `LEASES` is the money one.

### A process note worth keeping, because it is how a wrong answer was avoided

Two of that lens's forks misbehaved — nested forking failed inside them, so instead of reporting
their sub-slice each re-ran large parts of the whole task and wrote **directly to the shared output
file**, racing each other. The lens did not take the surviving file at face value: it re-verified
all 12 quotes against the pinned source (all matched), re-checked the severity downgrades against
real reachability, found that `busbar-kernel-egress/src/trust/` had never actually been read
(one fork admitted covering 330 of 1,701 lines), and independently added two findings itself.

That is the correct response to a subagent, and it is the same rule the house method states for
greps: **an agent's report is a lead, not a fact.** Had it accepted the file, the round would have
shipped a confident report over a slice nothing read.

---

## SECURITY: THE DATA PLANE FAILS OPEN, AND THE SPEND LANDS ON `anonymous`

`crates/busbar-kernel/src/auth/mod.rs:1868`, verified by hand:

```rust
let bindings = app.role_bindings.get(&module);
let gov_key = resolved.or_else(|| synthesize_principal_key(&principal, bindings));
if gov_key.is_none() && !principal.roles.is_empty() && bindings.is_some() {
    return Err(IdentityRefusal::NoGrant);       // <- three conditions, not one
}
Ok((AuthPrincipal(Some(principal)), GovCtx { key: gov_key }))
```

The refusal requires **all three** of: no governance key resolved, the principal carries roles, and
the module has a `role_bindings` table. So an IdP-authenticated caller who carries **no roles**, or
who arrives under a module with **no bindings table**, satisfies the first condition and fails the
other two — and is admitted with `GovCtx { key: None }`.

`key: None` downstream means **no pool ACL, no budget, and spend booked to the `anonymous` actor.**

The correct guard is the first condition alone: if no governance key resolved, refuse. The two
extra conjuncts only weaken it. This is simultaneously an authorization fail-open and a money
defect — unbudgeted spend, attributed to a principal that is not the one who spent it, which is
exactly the attribution a bank auditor checks first.

The finder read all four out-of-slice call sites to establish the consequence rather than assume
it, over a denominator of 99 files / 34,430 LOC with 49 read line-by-line.

---

## ROUND 2 — the remaining confirmed findings

Recorded compactly; each carries `file:line` and a positive control in
`~/.cache/busbar-codeaudit/findings/`.

| sev | where | finding |
|---|---|---|
| HIGH | `busbar-transport-http/src/transport.rs:128` | `HttpTransport::dial()` accepts any URI authority — **no IP-literal enforcement**, unlike sibling `busbar-transport-tls` which requires `authority.parse::<SocketAddr>()`. hyper's `HttpConnector` re-resolves a non-literal host at connect time **and on every pooled reconnect**, so this is a DNS-rebinding / TOCTOU SSRF backstop gap. A real construction site populates it from configured lane data (`busbar-llm/src/unit/route.rs:198`) |
| HIGH | `busbar-transport-grpc/src/client.rs:76`, `server.rs:88` | the HTTP/2 connection preface has **zero timeout** on both dial and accept, where `tls` has `HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT` and `ws` has `HANDSHAKE_BUDGET` — an unbounded slow-loris vector |
| HIGH | `busbar-kernel-ledger/src/identity.rs:83` | `residual`, the books-balance verifier, was left out of the saturating-guard conversion that `settle.rs` and `totals.rs` announce in their own headers. No `overflow-checks` in release ⇒ it **wraps** on exactly the pinned columns those files produce, and a maximally broken book verifies clean |
| MED | `busbar-transport-http/src/transport.rs:275` | no timeout bounds `client.request(req).await`; only TCP connect and HTTP/2 keepalive are bounded, so an HTTP/1.1 upstream that hangs after connecting is unbounded at this layer |
| MED | `busbar-kernel-scope/src/lib.rs:207` | the live admin authorization gate is a verbatim port of the 1.5.5 66-operation matrix, never extended for the 1.6.0 verb table. It disagrees with `busbar-core-admin`'s own `required_scope()` on exactly one row — `POST /admin/verify`, deliberately read-only at `verb.rs:719`, computed as `Full` by the gate. **Fails closed** (denies rather than over-grants), so it is a correctness defect, not a hole |
| MED | `busbar-mcp` `method.rs:1174`, `admin_view.rs:369` | both `quarantine_settle` call sites discard the durable write with `let _ =`. If that write fails as a rug-pull quarantine (or an operator's clearance) is decided, and the process restarts before the next successful settle, **the demotion silently reverts** with no error surfaced |
| MED | `busbar-a2a/src/a2a/receive.rs:2804` | the streaming relay swallows a failed durable write of the artifact resume cursor with a bare `let _ =` and no logging, while the structurally identical `transition` failure six lines above is logged with an error-once latch. `tasks/resubscribe` reads that cursor, so a store hiccup causes duplicate artifact redelivery with no trail |
| MED | `busbar-kernel-budget/src/decide.rs:283` | the concurrent-admission CAS loop, documented as guaranteeing racing admissions cannot jointly overshoot a cap, has **zero multi-threaded test**. Positive control: `HoldCell::take` and the breaker's single-flight probe both have real `thread::spawn` race tests |

### What the round REFUTED, which matters as much

- The egress pool's 8 `.expect()` mutex sites are a real idiom outlier (the house pattern
  `lock_recover` has 45 call sites) but **no panic is reachable** in any of the five critical
  sections: the one inner `.expect` is guarded two lines up, both `unreachable!()` are provably
  dead, the `Instant + Duration` overflow needs an operator-settable timeout that does not exist,
  and every `tokio::spawn` is inside a runtime. Downgraded to LOW. *Worth keeping because the blast
  radius, if one ever became reachable, is asymmetric:* `deliver_dial_outcome` disarms `DialGuard`
  only **after** taking the lock, so a panic at acquire leaves the guard armed and leaks
  `inflight_dials` permanently.
- Redirects are never followed by the HTTP egress client — structurally, not by policy.
- Request-smuggling defences in `raw.rs` are thorough (ambiguous CL/TE, obs-fold, non-final chunked
  all rejected); TLS verification and SPKI pinning have no production bypass; WS and gRPC
  compression are disabled at the dependency level, so there is no decompression-bomb surface.

## TWO WRITTEN BYTE-NEUTRALITY CLAIMS ARE FALSE — and each was the justification for a default

A 2×2 factorial over `root-admin` × `root-llm` isolated the C3 divergences perfectly — additive,
no interaction. Both features are default-ON, and the comment beside each says the measurement that
authorised that default. **Both measurements are false at HEAD, and I verified both comments myself.**

| cell | 1.5.5 | neither | `root-admin` | `root-llm` | both |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `billing\|rate-card\|history-mid-window` (`spend_final`) | 750,090,000 | 750,090,000 ✓ | 750,090,000 ✓ | **277,530,000 ✗** | **277,530,000 ✗** |
| `ledger\|amend\|adjusting-entries` | 404 | 404 ✓ | **403 ✗** | 404 ✓ | **403 ✗** |
| `ledger\|amend\|refused-unsigned` | 404 | 404 ✓ | **403 ✗** | 404 ✓ | **403 ✗** |
| `ledger\|rate-history\|as-of` | 200 | **400 ✗** | **400 ✗** | 200 ✓ | 200 ✓ |

**154 — `root-llm` moves money, and its own comment says it does not.** `crates/busbar/Cargo.toml:263`
reads *"the whole owed golden was re-run with it on and moved no cell — every money class
byte-identical, the llm, billing, route-failover and admin families at zero. That last measurement
is the one the default was waiting for."* Measured: it moves `billing` by **472,560,000 micro-units
on a single read.**

**155 — `root-admin` moves two owed ledger cells, and its own comment says it does not.**
`Cargo.toml:309` reads *"the 234 owed `admin.ops` cells and the whole 890-cell owed golden read the
same either way, which is the measurement the default was waiting for."* Measured: both
`ledger|amend` cells move 404→403, `not_found`→`forbidden`.

**156 — `root-llm` is load-bearing for CORRECTNESS, and the repo's own LLM-only gate builds without
it.** Without `root-llm`, `ledger|rate-history|as-of` answers **400 `invalid_request` where 1.5.5
answers 200**. And `scripts/proto-deletion-gate.sh:606` is
`MCP_KEEP="auth-admin-tokens,hooks-ranking,proto-llm"` — **`root-llm` is absent**. So that gate
builds, and passes judgement on, a binary with a broken rate-history endpoint.

### The shape, named — because it is not Law 8 and it is not Law 0

These are **measurements asserted in prose, recorded as settled, and re-run by nothing.** Each
comment states a denominator honestly (890 owed cells, 234 `admin.ops` cells) and says the
measurement was performed. Neither is wrong about what it measured; both are wrong about the tree
as it stands, because **the tree moved and no instrument re-ran them.** A fact written into a
comment is a snapshot with no expiry date on it.

**The remedy is mechanical and belongs in Phase 1:** any comment that authorises a default by
citing a measurement must name the command that reproduces it, and that command joins the gate set.
A default justified by an unreproducible measurement is a default justified by nothing.

### PARK-P1 IS SETTLED, DEFINITIVELY

`qa/evidence/1.6.0-parity-unblock.md` PARK-P1 offered two explanations for the rate-card cell and declined to
choose: a real pricing change, or clock skew. **The `root-admin` build reproduced the golden's
three figures exactly, ten minutes after the LLM-only build diverged, on the same host.**

*Clock skew cannot be switched off by a Cargo feature.* It is the code path. Supporting fact
already recorded: no cell in the corpus pins a clock, 0 of 2,318.

## C2 IS REFUTED — the export kind cannot be dropped in at all

Confirmed independently by two lenses that did not know about each other, each with positive
controls, and corroborated by a doc already in the tree.

C2 says *"every plugin compiles in OR drops in — same contract, same loading path"* (Part 2 #2,
the one universal rule). For `kind: export` the drop-in fold does not exist:

- `config/mod.rs:1983` — `resolve_export()` matches `def.module.trim()` against the four built-ins
  in `sections.rs:435` and **hard-errors at boot on anything else.** There is no fallback arm
  delegating to the plugin registry, so an `export.<name>.module:` naming a dropped-in plugin
  cannot be written in a config file at all.
- `plugin-loader/src/registry.rs:398` — `open_export` has **zero callers anywhere in the tree.**
  Positive controls under the identical search: `open_auth`, `open_hook`, `open_secret` and
  `open_store` all have real production call sites.
- `migrate_export.rs` carries the same closed vocabulary, so the migration tool is blind the same way.
- `plugin_routes.rs:115` — `ExportDispatch` is `#[allow(dead_code)]` and never constructed.
- `qa/evidence/1.6.0-config-surface.md:888` **already flags this exact gap**, as
  "DOCUMENTED-NOT-IMPLEMENTED". It was known and never became an item.

So the compiled-in fold serves four modules and the dropped-in fold is unreachable by construction.
**That is not a gap in a ratchet; it is the claim being false for one of the seven kinds** — and
the instrument that should catch it is the same `INSTANCE_VOCAB_KINDS` hole that hides the C1
breach. One root cause, two refuted claims, recorded once in `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` Part 1.

## NOT ON THE LIST — found by running the tests, not by reading them

The full-workspace run produced **27 unique failures: 13 real defects across 6 root causes**,
2 stale tests, 12 harness. **Four of the six root causes were on no item.**

| finding | evidence | why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| **`busbar-contract` declares a `test-seal` feature**, breaking the one-surface invariant (landed in `217b21cc0`) | 5 tests, incl. `the_crate_declares_no_features`; takes `feature-sets` RED on the real tree, which cascades into 3 more | **largest single failing cluster in the release, and `grep -i test-seal` on the todo returned 0 hits** |
| **`qa/instance-noun-neutrality.toml:2753` names a path the tree no longer has** — `engine_kit.rs` is now a directory | the `[[leak]]` row scans **zero files and PASSES** | a textbook false zero, inside the C1 census |
| **the walk floor has lost its own proof** — `walk(min_files=1)` on an empty set returns `WalkError::Empty`, not `BelowFloor` | `infra.rs:192` | the `min_files` floor is the single most-repeated safety device in the gate library, and it is now unproven |
| **the audit-register trio** read a file this session deleted | `cli.rs:90`, `readers.rs:59`, `:106` | **mine — fixed in `2a0a832ff`**, with the writer's byte-for-byte parity proof repointed rather than dropped |
| `cargo test -p busbar-voice --features runtime` **does not compile** — 12 × `E0407` in `testkit/fixture_host.rs` | verified on a clean worktree, so committed | the Voice workflow's `boot-validate` job runs this verbatim; `cargo test --workspace` never sees it because it builds default features only |
| **5 MCP client-role hostile-server findings** | `CLI.HOSTILE.{EVIL-ELICITATION-PASSWORD, MRTR-UNDECLARED-CAPABILITY, RUGPULL-SCHEMA-CHANGE, OUTPUTSCHEMA-VIOLATION-DETECTED, BAD-ICON-SCHEME}` | busbar-as-client does not detect a rug-pull, an undeclared capability, an outputSchema violation, a bad icon scheme, or an evil elicitation prompt. **Security, and 0 hits on the todo** |
| **`llm-conformance` is red on EVERY branch** | pinned Gemini discovery doc drifted upstream (`836bf6ea…` → `fe47cc95…`); `validate.py` then refuses to judge anything | one `vendor.sh --repin gemini` away, after reviewing the upstream diff. Note the drift DETECTOR is the one case that passes — it works; everything downstream of it is starved |
| **the voice `tool-reply` leg is run by no workflow**, and the anti-vacuity lint structurally cannot see it | `verdict-covers-every-leg.py` checks the verdict job depends on every *job*; a leg with no job at all is invisible | Law 0 again: the coverage lint's denominator is jobs, not legs |

### And the best-diagnosed harness bug of the round

**8 × `root::units_llm::tests::*` fail only when `root::plane_decision` runs in the same process** —
including under `--test-threads=1`, so it is ordering, not concurrency. Root cause:
`plane/registry.rs`'s `TEST_MEMO` is keyed on `(installed_len, registered_len)`, so **two different
registration sets of equal size alias.** A decision-only fold cached under `(N,1)` is served to a
later `registered = [llm]` — also length 1 — the LLM plane vanishes from `plane_decls()`, and every
route 404s.

The comment above that memo reasons carefully about why a *sum* would alias and why the *pair*
fixes it. **The pair does not fix the equal-length case, which is the one that bites.** The tests'
own comments say they were written to catch exactly this collapse — *"green on six identical 404s
and prove nothing about the loop at all"* — so the suite anticipated the failure mode and the
cache defeated it.

## THE MONEY AUTHORITY WAS FLIPPED WITHOUT THE PROOF THAT GATES IT — and it likely explains the C3 money cells

This is the top finding of the session. Every step verified by hand.

**Decision #29 says the per-plane money-authority flip onto the unified kernel loop lands only when
that plane's money family is fleet-box oracle-green.** Four places in the tree state the flip has
not happened:

- `main.rs:635` — *"DORMANT — it registers ZERO planes, so every gauntlet/session path stays on the
  substrate loop, byte-identical."*
- `gauntlet_install.rs` module doc — *"DORMANT: `install` registers ZERO planes."*
- `a2a_kernel_rider.rs:16` — *"DORMANT — DUAL-PATH, MONEY AUTHORITY NOT FLIPPED."*
- `units_llm.rs:1931` — *"IT IS DORMANT. Nothing mounts it, and it is NOT the shipped authority."*

**All four are false.** `install()` is called unconditionally at `main.rs:638` and its body flips
all four planes:

```rust
pub fn install() {
    #[cfg(feature = "plane-mcp")]  flip_one_shot_to_kernel(busbar_mcp::PLANE_KEY);
    #[cfg(feature = "plane-a2a")]  flip_one_shot_to_kernel(busbar_a2a::PLANE_KEY);
    #[cfg(feature = "proto-llm")]  flip_one_shot_to_kernel(busbar_llm::PLANE_DECL.key);
    #[cfg(feature = "plane-voice")] flip_session_to_kernel(busbar_voice::PLANE_KEY);
}
```

Every one of those `cfg`s is **on by default** (`busbar/Cargo.toml:192`).

**The mechanism, which is what makes the "dormant" claims sound plausible and wrong.** The riders
say *"the shipped authority stays `run_gauntlet`"* — true, and irrelevant, because `run_gauntlet`
**is** the seam. `plane_host/mod.rs:2394`:

```rust
let selected = plane.capability_key().and_then(session_runner);
if let Some(runner) = selected { return runner(req, plane); }
```

`flip_*_to_kernel` populates exactly that registry. So calling `run_gauntlet` now dispatches into
the kernel loop for all four planes. The same file confirms the other half at `:2398`, referring
to *"the (now-deleted) substrate `admit_open`"* — **the substrate loop this plan believes is the
live money path no longer exists.**

### Why this matters more than a stale comment

`gauntlet_install.rs`'s own function doc asserts the flip is *"proven byte-identical (the
shadow-compare rider tests + the fleet-box oracle, #29)."*

**The oracle run in this session measures 16 diverging cells, two of them money** — including
`billing|key-usage|after-upstream-down` reading `spend 250 → 0` and `tokens 18 → 0`. The proof
#29 names as the precondition for this flip does not hold on the binary that has already been
flipped.

**This is the leading explanation for the two money divergences**, and it is testable rather than
speculative: the money-settlement path moved from the substrate loop to the kernel teller loop
under a byte-identity claim, and the oracle now reports money figures that differ. It also fits the
abandoned-hold family — the kernel loop is where `Abandoned::drop`, the unwired sweep and the
never-called `recover_all` live.

**The first action is not a fix, it is a measurement:** re-run the two divergent money cells against
a build with the flip reverted (comment out the four lines in `install()`). If they return to the
1.5.5 figures, the flip is the cause and #29's gate was load-bearing exactly as written. That is
one build and two cells, and it decides how much of Phase 2 and Phase 3 is one root cause.

### Corroborating: the metering that does not happen

`grep -rn -e meter_ledger -e record_usage crates/busbar-mcp/ crates/busbar-a2a/` returns **nothing**
(control: the same grep over `busbar-llm`/`busbar-voice` returns 9 files). MCP and A2A admit through
`GovState::try_admit` and meter `UsageComponent::Queries` at amount **0**, which `token_usage_for`
maps to `None`. With `per_request_fee` defaulting to 0, **a `budget:` cap is unreachable by those
two planes entirely** — while A2A's own refusal text says *"this key's budget is spent."*

And `Door::record_usage` in `decide.rs` has **zero non-test callers**, so the new admission stack
charges requests and ledgers nothing.

---

## THE WORST ONE: TWO ADMISSION DOORS ANSWER THE SAME QUESTION IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS

Verified by hand, both sides, at HEAD. Decision #42 says an unpriced model must REFUSE, never
silently price at zero. There are two live doors and they disagree:

```rust
// crates/busbar-kernel/src/cost.rs:755        THE LIVE LLM ADMISSION GATE
for (model, units) in models {
    if let Some(rate) = self.rate_for(model) {          // no rate -> body never runs
        nanos = nanos.saturating_add(rate.reserved_nanos(units));
    }                                                    // ...the model's WHOLE spend is dropped
}

// crates/busbar-kernel-budget/src/price.rs:230          THE BUDGET DOOR
for (model, units) in models {
    if self.model_unpriced(model) { return i64::MAX; }   // blocks, per #42
```

**The kernel door is live on the admission path** — `governance/state.rs:1613`, `:1625`, `:1691`
and `:1970` all call it.

**The attack is configuration, not code.** Send traffic to a model the rate card does not name.
Its consumption derives as **zero**, the budget cap never trips, and spend is unbounded on the
plane that carries essentially all revenue. The same arm at `:783` feeds the operator's
"spend so far" and "budget remaining" figures, so the operator's dashboard agrees that nothing was
spent.

`qa/evidence/1.6.0-money-sweep.md:752` **named both sites.** One was fixed and one was not, which
is what turns this from a standing PARK into a live regression: the tree now holds two doors
answering one #42 input in opposite directions, and the wrong one is the one on the revenue path.

### And the books-balance verifier wraps

`busbar-kernel-ledger/src/identity.rs:83` — `residual`, the function that checks the books balance —
was left out of the saturating-guard conversion that `settle.rs` and `totals.rs` each announce in
their own file headers. Release builds set no `overflow-checks`, so it **wraps** on exactly the
pinned columns those files deliberately produce. A maximally broken book verifies clean.

That is the third instance in this section of the same shape: **the money is wrong AND the thing
that checks the money cannot say no.** Reconciliation compares 0 to 0 and agrees; the tamper
detector is never constructed; the balance verifier wraps to zero. Under the owner's standing
standard this is the top of Phase 2.

---

## THE MONEY FINDING — the hold/settle/recover protocol is half-built, and the check that would notice passes at 0 == 0

Four independent audit lenses, plus the durability sweep, converged on one picture. None of them
knew what the others were looking at. Every line below carries a `file:line` and was verified.

### 1. A hold is never written down

`recovery.rs:35` states that open holds are journalled and recovered. They are not. **All six
production `Entry::new` sites are at or after SETTLE time**, so no hold is ever journalled. The
`Transaction = 1` record class documented to carry holds is real — the class is not the defect;
nothing writes one.

### 2. Nothing recovers them, and the recovery code is production-compiled

`recover_all` has **zero production callers** — the entire `recovery` module is compiled into the
shipping binary and referenced only from one integration test. Positive control: `open_store` is
production at `appbuild.rs:1160` under the identical search.

### 3. Both replay paths filter out everything except migrations

`durability.rs:232` and `:637` are the only two `replay()` calls in the tree, and both filter
`RecordClass::Migration` alone.

### 4. THE ONE THAT MATTERS: the money view resets to zero, and the reconciliation PASSES

`durability.rs:773` builds `Ledger::dual_writing(legacy_rows)` — an **empty book** — with
`checkpoints: Vec::new()`, on the line after the recovered journal opens. Combined with (3), the
money view resets to zero on every restart **and the reconciliation identity passes, because both
sides are zero.**

That is Law 0 inside the money path: *an instrument that cannot produce a NO is not a check.*
A reconciliation that compares 0 to 0 and reports agreement is the most dangerous green in the
release, because it is the check a bank auditor would ask for first.

### 5. The sweep half of the protocol was never built

Nothing calls `mark()`, `touch()`, `sweep` or `sweep_settle` in production. The drop guard
(`units_llm.rs:989-993`) **removes the slot instead of marking it**, so a sweeper could not see it
even if one were wired. A client disconnect mid-dispatch silently skips a charge.

`HoldCell::take` compounds it: its doc claims **two** callers, the gate spec says **three**, and
there are three (`teller.rs:1207`, `teller.rs:1305`, `tick.rs:289`). The CI gate that exists to
guarantee *"no fourth anywhere"* scans for a literal substring that **provably cannot see the third
site** — positive control: the same grep matches `teller.rs` twice and `tick.rs` zero times against
a real `take` call there.

### 6. Two durability defects that lose settled money, not just held money

- `replay()` scans **only the current segment**, so the migration marker becomes unreachable after
  the first 64 MiB roll.
- `DirectoryFactory::open` **never fsyncs the holding directory on a roll**. A new segment's
  directory entry is not durable, so a segment's settlements can vanish *after* `sync_data()`
  returned success. This is the classic fsync-the-parent-directory omission and it loses data that
  the code has already told the caller is safe.

### 7. Charged for work that was refused

`busbar-a2a/src/a2a/receive.rs:2085` meters the hop and stamps `OUTCOME_APPLIED` — and **five**
pre-flight gates that can still abort the hop are checked afterwards, at `:2131-2275`, including
the breaker's own open-circuit refusal. With a tripped breaker the caller is billed for a hop
busbar decided never to attempt. `breaker_fastfail_tests.rs` exercises exactly this and asserts
nothing about the ledger; `metered_verbs_tests.rs` proves the suite checks ledger rows when the
authors meant to.

### 8. The tamper check cannot fire

`busbar-kernel-ledger/src/verify.rs:35` declares `Finding::AnchorHeadDiffers` — the
checkpoint-versus-anchor rollback detector — renders it, and **never constructs it**: `verify()`
takes no anchor parameter at all. Positive control: the other five `Finding` variants are all
constructed and asserted in `checkpoint_tests.rs`.

### WHAT THIS MEANS

Taken together: **money is held in memory only, never journalled, never recovered, never swept,
and the restart-time check that should catch the loss compares an empty book against an empty
book and reports agreement.** The abandoned-hold defect found earlier is not an isolated bug — it
is the visible corner of a protocol that was designed, documented, and left unwired.

Under the owner's standing standard — *"would it pass a bank auditor? if not it is wrong, fix
it"* — this is a NAY, and it is the largest single body of work in Phase 2.

**These are not self-approvable and not owner questions either.** They are defects with a clear
right answer; the owner's standing money authority says fix them. The only items that go to the
owner are the two oracle cells whose FIGURES changed, above.

## C3 IS MEASURED, AND IT IS REFUTED — 16 cells, 12 of them product

The LLM-only build now exists and has been recorded against the published 1.5.5 golden. C3 was
"unevidenced" this morning; it is now **falsified with a number**.

```
owed 916 · diverging 16 · accepted 289 · unbaselined 1402 · weighted D/W 0.0144 · --strict rc=1
golden 84bde0a0… (published 1.5.5)      candidate 8b46bdc2… (LLM-only)
```

C3's own refutation criterion is *"one unexplained cell."* There are sixteen. Four are
environmental (no MySQL/Postgres/Valkey on this host — golden PASS, candidate SKIP), leaving
**12 product divergences**.

**The builds all compile.** Set L (LLM-only, shipped paths) 16,710,080 bytes · Set M (strict
minimum) 15,848,512 · DEFAULT (all planes) 22,488,240 — the all-planes binary carries **+41.9%**
over the strict minimum.

**CORRECTION TO MY OWN EARLIER FRAMING.** I wrote that every previous C3 recording was taken
against a binary 6.6 MB larger than the one C3 is a claim about, and implied that mattered. Both
legs have now been recorded and it did not: **the 16 diverging cells are the SAME 16 on both
builds** (D/W 0.0144 vs 0.0152). Across all 913 cells both recorded, the two builds differ on
exactly **two** — `admin.ops|DeleteOverlaySection|not-found` (4 overlay sections in 1.5.5, 6 in
LLM-only, 8 in all-planes, the extra two being MCP's `tools` and A2A's `agents`) and
`boot.refusal|BOOT-181|boot` (all-planes emits one extra WARN line, which retires the prior
document's PARK-P6 by showing it is plane-dependent). Recording the wrong binary was a real defect
in the method; it masked **zero** divergences. The divergences are HEAD's, not the feature set's.

### THE TWO MONEY CELLS — PARKED FOR THE OWNER, never self-approved (#10/#59)

| cell | 1.5.5 | 1.6.0 LLM-only |
|---|---|---|
| `billing\|rate-card\|history-mid-window` | `/spend_after_a` 25,000,000 · `/spend_after_b` 500,060,000 · `/spend_final` 750,090,000 | **2,500,000 · 27,500,000 · 277,530,000** (across `by_key`, `by_model`, `total`) |
| `billing\|key-usage\|after-upstream-down` | `/spend_cents` 250 · `/tokens` 18 | **0 · 0** |

**CORRECTION — I read this one backwards the first time.** I recorded `spend 250 → 0` as money
disappearing. The cell's own `why` says **spend 0 is the CORRECT answer**: an upstream that went
down served nothing, so nothing is owed. **1.5.5 was the one that was wrong**, billing 250 cents
for a failed request, and 1.6.0 fixes it. That makes it C4-over-C3 — the money rule beating the
byte-identity rule — and what is missing is not a fix but a **signed exception that was never
written.** It is still an owner item, for the opposite reason: a customer-visible improvement that
changes billing and has no recorded sign-off.

**The first cell settles a question the prior document could not.** `qa/evidence/1.6.0-parity-unblock.md`
PARK-P1 offered two explanations and declined to choose: (a) a real pricing change, or (b) clock
skew, since the cell pins no clock. The measured triple is **digit-for-digit the priced-at-charge
answer that cell's own `why` names**, against a design intent that "ONLY the final read moves."
Clock skew does not reproduce three exact predicted constants. **It is (a).** And the tiebreak is
now general: **no cell in the corpus pins a clock — 0 of 2,318** — so (b) was never specific to
this cell and cannot be offered again for any cell.

### THE OTHER TEN

Ledger authorization (2): `ledger|amend|adjusting-entries` and `|refused-unsigned` move 404→403,
`not_found`→`forbidden` — an error-shape change on a signed surface.
Admin surface (4): `GetAuth|ok`, `GetConfig|ok`, `PostConfigReload|ok` flip `auth.open` true→false;
`GetAudit|ok` gains `/items/*/scheme` null→2.
Audit (1): `teller|audit-record`, the same new `scheme` field on a second surface.
Boot diagnostics (2): `BOOT-181` prints its TLS error **twice in one line**; `BOOT-135` says
"incomplete deflate stream" where 1.5.5 said "unexpected end of file".
Config grammar (1): `neutrality|NEUT-U-auth|validate` lists `operator_pub` among `auth:`'s expected
fields — a key with **zero** hits at v1.5.5 (positive control: `signing_key` has 7).

**Three of these are HEAD movement, not plane removal.** The `auth.open` and `GetAudit`
divergences are byte-identical between the LLM-only and all-planes builds, so they arrived since
the 2026-09-22 measurement and have nothing to do with C3's compile-out.

### A NEW INSTRUMENT DEFECT — the register forgives on a premise that is false for this build

The LLM-only binary still serves an admin OpenAPI document containing `A2aTrustView` and the
MCP/A2A endpoints, because the document is `include_bytes!("openapi.json.gz")` — compile-time
embedded and **entirely feature-independent**. The register forgives this under **F-011b**,
*"additive — openapi.json describes the MCP and A2A endpoints"* — a rationale premised on those
planes existing. With them compiled out, the build **advertises a surface it cannot serve**, and
the neutrality cell whose stated contract is *"the document must list only the 1.5.5 admin
surface"* is scored `accepted` rather than `diverging`.

This is Law 0 in the register rather than in a gate: **a forgiveness whose premise is not checked
is not a forgiveness, it is a blindfold.** Every `accepted-differences.json` entry needs its
premise stated as a condition the runner can evaluate against the build under test.

### THE DIFFERENTIAL ALREADY WORKS

Of the 235 cells both builds have recorded, exactly **one** differs between them:
`admin.ops|DeleteOverlaySection|not-found` — 1.5.5 lists 4 overlay sections, LLM-only lists 6, the
default build lists 8, the extra two being MCP's `tools` and A2A's `agents`. The all-planes build's
additional surface, caught in a single cell. That is the instrument C3 always needed.

### CORRECTION TO THIS PLAN'S OWN CLAIM

I recorded earlier that no divergence list existed. One does: `qa/evidence/1.6.0-parity-unblock.md` §5 parks
**8**, measured 2026-09-22 on an all-planes build against a golden owing **913**, not the committed
916. The accurate statement is narrower and still holds: **the list for the binary C3 is actually
about has never existed, it is twice as long, and it is measured here against the committed golden.**
